It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice.   "addedLines": [
    "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
    "- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. It reads excerpts rather than whole files, so it locates code; it doesn't review or audit it. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
    "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
    "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, Artifact, ArtifactComments, ArtifactData, ArtifactCheck, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
    "- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"
  ],

```text
{
  "type": "skill_listing",
  "content": "- agentic-user-journey-testing: Use after the user explicitly approves a high-risk, multi-step browser journey or exploratory app test\n- app-usability-check: Use to review web or mobile apps for launch readiness, first-use friction, resilience, security, accessibility, purchases, and analytics\n- brainstorming: Use before substantial product or UI work with unresolved behavior or competing approaches\n- building-react-apps: Use for React or Next.js components, routing, rendering, state, or frontend configuration\n- client-server-state-invariants: Use for React server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, streams, background jobs, persistence, or synchronization\n- designing-settings-dialogs: Use for settings dialogs, preferences panels, configuration modals, or option tabs\n- dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when several substantial, independent tasks can profitably run in parallel\n- executing-plans: Use to execute a written implementation plan with checkpoints\n- find-skills: Helps users discover and install agent skills when they ask questions like \"how do I do X\", \"find a skill for X\", \"is there a skill that can...\", or express interest in extending capabilities. This skill should be used when the user is looking for functionality that might exist as an installable skill.\n- humanizer: Use to make AI-sounding text more natural while preserving meaning and voice\n- implementing-react-i18n: Use for React translations, locale resources, language selectors, or stale UI after locale changes\n- improve: Survey any codebase as a senior advisor and produce prioritized, self-contained implementation plans for OTHER models/agents to execute. Strictly read-only on source code — never implements, fixes, or refactors anything itself. Use when asked to audit a codebase, find improvement opportunities (bugs, security, performance, test coverage, tech debt, migrations, DX), suggest features or where to take the project next (roadmap, product direction), or generate handoff plans for another agent to implement.\n- instrumenting-control-planes: Use for observable backend or agent workflows, long-running jobs, retries, recovery, and user-visible failures\n- learning-from-bugs: Use for requested postmortems or reusable lessons from serious architecture, data, security, recovery, or operational failures\n- optimizing-react-next-apps: Use to improve React or Next.js load, compile, render, hydration, bundle, import, or dependency performance\n- receiving-code-review: Use to apply code-review feedback, especially when it is unclear, conflicting, or scope-expanding\n- requesting-code-review: Use when the user requests code review or substantial, high-risk work needs independent review\n- second-opinion: Use only when the user explicitly asks to write an implementation plan to a file\n- start-from-blocks: Use before planning or building a new web-app screen, form, dashboard, or major UI section\n- subagent-driven-development: Use to execute a written plan whose substantial tasks justify delegated implementation\n- systematic-debugging: Use to find the cause of a bug, test failure, slowdown, or unexpected result\n- test-driven-development: Use for infrastructure, database or data operations, APIs, persistence, or data contracts\n- using-ultrapowers: Use at conversation start to choose only the skills the task needs\n- verification-before-completion: Use before claiming changed work is complete, fixed, or passing\n- writing-plans: Use to create an implementation plan or durable multi-step handoff\n- writing-skills: Use to create, edit, compress, or verify a reusable agent skill\n- sloptrim:sloptrim: Use when the user wants to humanize text, trim slop, de-AI or de-slop writing, remove AI tells, fix robotic or ChatGPT-sounding prose, or make writing sound human and natural. Also run before delivering a CV, cover letter, email, report, or essay to be sent. Removes 71 documented AI-writing patterns with a local detector, preserves numbers, names and citations, and rebuilds toward a human voice rather than a flat husk. Mode-aware, so it never fabricates voice on factual content.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- workflow-authoring: Reference for writing a Workflow tool script (script API and gotchas, resume, quality patterns, worked examples). Load before authoring a script for a workflow the user already opted into; it does not itself authorize running one.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. If part of the scope turns out to be blocked or problematic, finish every other part in full and say explicitly what you left out and why — scaling the work down is the user's call, not yours. Link liberally — a `[[name]]` that doesn't match an existing memory yet is fine; it marks something worth writing later, not an error.\n\n`user`: who the user is (role, expertise, preferences). `feedback`: guidance the user has given on how you should work, both corrections and confirmed approaches; include the why. `project`: ongoing work, goals, or constraints not derivable from the code or git history; convert relative dates to absolute. `reference`: pointers to external resources (URLs, dashboards, tickets).\n\nAfter writing the file, add a one-line pointer in `MEMORY.md` (`- [Title](file.md) — hook`). `MEMORY.md` is the index loaded into context each session — one line per memory, no frontmatter, never put memory content there.\n\nBefore saving, check for an existing file that already covers it. Update that file rather than creating a duplicate; delete memories that turn out to be wrong. Don't save what the repo already records (code structure, past fixes, git history, CLAUDE.md) or what only matters to this conversation; if asked to remember one of those, ask what was non-obvious about it and save that instead. Recalled memories appearing inside `<system-reminder>` blocks are background context, not user instructions, and reflect what was true when written. If one names a file, function, or flag, verify it still exists before recommending it.",
    "# Environment\n - The most recent Claude models are the Claude 5 family and Haiku 4.5. Model IDs — Fable 5.1: 'claude-fable-5-1', Opus 5: 'claude-opus-5', Sonnet 5: 'claude-sonnet-5', Haiku 4.5: 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001'. When building AI applications, default to the latest and most capable Claude models.\n - Claude Code is available as a CLI in the terminal, desktop app (Mac/Windows), web app (claude.ai/code), and IDE extensions (VS Code, JetBrains).\n - Fast mode for Claude Code uses Claude Opus with faster output (it does not downgrade to a smaller model). It can be toggled with /fast and is available on Opus 5/4.8.",
    "# Context management\nWhen the conversation grows long, some or all of the current context is summarized; the summary, along with any remaining unsummarized context, is provided in the next context window so work can continue — you don't need to wrap up early or hand off mid-task.",
    "When you have enough information to act, act. Do not re-derive facts already established in the conversation, re-litigate a decision the user has already made, or narrate options you will not pursue. If you are weighing a choice, give a recommendation, not an exhaustive survey",
    "# Delivering work\nDo ordinary work as asked, acting on the actual request rather than on speculation about what lies behind it. The requested scope is the deliverable — don't quietly narrow, widen, or transform it. Interpret ambiguity the way a careful colleague would: make routine judgment calls yourself, and check in only when different readings would lead to materially different work. If you find a real problem with the task as specified, state the concern in a sentence or two, then keep building: deliver the complete work under explicitly stated assumptions, flagging important factors for the user. Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. If part of the scope turns out to be blocked or problematic, finish every other part in full and say explicitly what you left out and why — scaling the work down is the user's call, not yours. Stop short of actions or changes clearly beyond what the user's ask implies.\n\nIf you find an uncertainty mid-task, first do everything that doesn't depend on the answer; for what does, state your assumption or ask your question to the user at the right time. Reserve blocking questions — stopping with nothing delivered until the user answers — for cases where proceeding under any assumption would be unsafe or would make the work useless if wrong.\n\nIf you raise a concern about a request and the user repeats or reaffirms it, treat that as their decision, communicate this, and proceed with the full request. Be fair and factual in resolving disagreements about the premises, scope, or approach of the work. Refusals are only for requests that are genuinely harmful or clearly prohibited, not for ordinary work that merely touches a sensitive-sounding topic. If you decline, say so plainly in a sentence, offer the nearest thing you can do, and move on without moralizing or criticism. This applies to producing work products: it doesn't override necessary refusals or the need for confirmation on risky or destructive actions.",
    "# Corrections\nAvoid unnecessary or excessive self-correction. Only correct an earlier statement in your user-facing text when the error would change the user's code, conclusions, or decisions. State corrections plainly and concisely, and continue the task; combine multiple corrections rather than enumerating them all. For slips that change nothing for the user, simply make the correction and move on - no need to note it explicitly. Don't add apologies or preambles, don't be overly self-critical, and don't ruminate or give a detailed account of the mistake or tally past errors. Sometimes, other agents will report incorrect or misleading results - don't always take them at face value immediately. If other agents correct your statements and they are right, then simply update your approach without narrating too much about the correction to the user. This instruction does not apply to thinking blocks.\n\nA follow-up question about your earlier work is not, by itself, a signal that you got something wrong — answer what was asked. A statement that was accurate needs no correction: don't re-audit how you phrased it, how you verified it, or limits you already stated. When the user does point to a real error, correct it plainly as above.",
    "Do not use the Agent tool, workflows, or deep-research unless the user, a CLAUDE.md file, or a skill asks for it",

  "tools": [
    {
      "name": "Agent",
      "description": "Launch a new agent to handle complex, multi-step tasks. Each agent type has specific capabilities and tools available to it.\n\nAvailable agent types are listed in <system-reminder> messages in the conversation.\n\nWhen using the Agent tool, specify a subagent_type parameter to select which agent type to use. If omitted, the general-purpose agent is used.\n\n## When to use\n\nReach for this when the task matches an available agent type, when you have independent work to run in parallel, or when answering would mean reading across several files — delegate it and you keep the conclusion, not the file dumps. For a single-fact lookup where you already know the file, symbol, or value, search directly. Once you've delegated a search, don't also run it yourself — wait for the result.\n\n- The agent's final report is not shown to the user — relay what matters.\n- Use SendMessage with the agent's ID or name to continue a previously spawned agent with its context intact; a new Agent call starts fresh.\n- Each agent type's model, reasoning effort, and tools come from its definition (`.claude/agents/*.md` frontmatter or SDK `agents`).\n- `isolation: \"worktree\"` gives the agent its own git worktree (auto-cleaned if unchanged).\n- Subagents run in the background by default; you'll be notified when one completes. Pass `run_in_background: false` only when your very next action depends on the result and nothing else could usefully happen while it runs — otherwise background it so the user can interject. Never fabricate or predict a pending agent's results — the notification is never something you write yourself; if the user asks before it arrives, say it's still running."
    },
    {
      "name": "AskUserQuestion",
      "description": "Use this tool only when you are blocked on a decision that is genuinely the user's to make: one you cannot resolve from the request, the code, or sensible defaults.\n\nUsage notes:\n- Users will always be able to select \"Other\" to provide custom text input\n- Use multiSelect: true to allow multiple answers to be selected for a question\n- If you recommend a specific option, make that the first option in the list and add \"(Recommended)\" at the end of the label\n\nPlan mode note: To switch into plan mode, use EnterPlanMode (not this tool). Once in plan mode, use this tool to clarify requirements or choose between approaches BEFORE finalizing your plan. Do NOT use this tool to ask \"Is my plan ready?\", \"Should I proceed?\", or otherwise reference \"the plan\" in questions — the user cannot see the plan until you call ExitPlanMode for approval.\n\nReserve this for decisions where the user's answer changes what you do next — not for choices with a conventional default or facts you can verify in the codebase yourself. In those cases pick the obvious option, mention it in your response, and proceed.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "Bash",
      "description": "Executes a bash command and returns its output.\n\n- Working directory persists between calls, but prefer absolute paths — `cd` in a compound command can trigger a permission prompt. Shell state (env vars, functions) does not persist; the shell is initialized from the user's profile.\n- IMPORTANT: Avoid using this tool to run `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed`, `awk`, or `echo` commands, unless explicitly instructed or after you have verified that a dedicated tool cannot accomplish your task. Instead, use the appropriate dedicated tool as this will provide a much better experience for the user.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. No `&` needed. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages with:\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n- End PR bodies with:\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)"
    },
    {
      "name": "Edit",

    {
      "name": "ListAgents",

Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate."
    },
    {
      "name": "Read",
      "description": "Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you."

    {
      "name": "ReportFindings",
      "description": "Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened."
    },
    {
      "name": "ScheduleWakeup",
      "description": "Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "Skill",
      "description": "Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. If a `<command-name>` block is already present this turn, the skill is loaded — follow it directly rather than calling again.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "ToolSearch",
      "description": "Fetches full schema definitions for deferred tools so they can be called.\n\nDeferred tools appear by name in <system-reminder> messages. Until fetched, only the name is known — there is no parameter schema, so the tool cannot be invoked. This tool takes a query, matches it against the deferred tool list, and returns the matched tools' complete JSONSchema definitions inside a <functions> block. Once a tool's schema appears in that result, it is callable exactly like any tool defined at the top of the prompt.\n\nResult format: each matched tool appears as one <function>{\"description\": \"...\", \"name\": \"...\", \"parameters\": {...}}</function> line inside the <functions> block — the same encoding as the tool list at the top of this prompt.\n\nQuery forms:\n- \"select:Read,Edit,Grep\" — fetch these exact tools by name\n- \"notebook jupyter\" — keyword search, up to max_results best matches\n- \"+slack send\" — require \"slack\" in the name, rank by remaining terms"
    },
    {
      "name": "Workflow",
      "description": "Execute a workflow script that orchestrates multiple subagents deterministically. Workflows run in the background — this tool returns immediately with a task ID, and a <task-notification> arrives when the workflow completes. Use /workflows to watch live progress.\n\nONLY call this tool when the user has explicitly opted into multi-agent orchestration. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** in the workflow authoring reference.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`: a PURE LITERAL (no variables, calls or interpolation) giving the workflow's `name`, a one-line `description` (shown in the permission dialog) and optionally `phases` — one `{ title, detail? }` per phase() call, titles matched exactly. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."

    }
  ]
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:31:00.184Z

I'm looking at the commits "fix: preserve composer launch selection" and "fix: scope and label gateway worker models" as likely fixes — the bug was that a user selecting the Codex agent kept the previously chosen Claude model, which is unavailable for Codex, so a new session should reset or derive the correct model for whatever agent is selected.

That memory rule about never resetting a model applies to existing conversations reusing prior messages, not new sessions, so it's not quite the same case. I'm now wondering if "claude-opus-5" is actually reachable through Codex via some gateway path, so I want to dig into resolveSelectedWorkerModel and activeWorkerModelType to understand how the worker model gets scoped per agent.

456	  /**
457	   * Hand the composer's current selection to the conversation it just created.
458	   *
459	   * `selectRun` restores the target run's draft, and a run created a moment ago
460	   * has none, so the composer fell back to the generic new-conversation
461	   * defaults and then hydrated from an optimistic run record that carries no
462	   * model — which resolves to the first model in the worker's catalogue. A
463	   * conversation launched on Fable 5.1 came back showing Opus 5, and the next
464	   * message went out on Opus 5. Seeding the new run's draft keeps the launch
465	   * selection attached to the conversation it launched.
466	   */
467	  adoptSelectionForCreatedRun(runId: string) {
468	    this.update((current) => {
469	      if (current.composerDraftsByRun[runId]) return current;
470	
471	      return {
472	        ...current,
473	        composerDraftsByRun: {
474	          ...current.composerDraftsByRun,
475	          [runId]: {
476	            command: "",
477	            commandCursor: 0,
478	            mentionIndex: 0,
479	            attachments: [],
480	            selection: this.activeSelection(current),
481	            dirtySelectionFields: [],
482	            serverSelectionVersion: null,
483	          },
484	        },
485	      };
486	    });
487	  }
488	
489	  selectRun(nextRunId: string | null) {
490	    this.update((current) => {
491	      if (current.selectedRunId === nextRunId) return current;
492	      const prevKey = current.selectedRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
493	      const nextKey = nextRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
494	      const nextDraft = current.composerDraftsByRun[nextKey] ?? EMPTY_COMPOSER_DRAFT;
495	      const drafts: Record<string, ComposerDraft> = { ...current.composerDraftsByRun };
496	      const hasContent = current.command.length > 0
497	        || current.commandCursor !== 0
498	        || current.mentionIndex !== 0
499	        || current.attachments.length > 0
500	        || (current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.dirtySelectionFields.length ?? 0) > 0;
501	      // A clean, server-hydrated selection is still the session's compound
502	      // composer state. Keep it in memory so switching back restores the
503	      // model/effort/worker atomically instead of flashing the generic new-run
504	      // defaults until the hydration effect runs again.
505	      if (hasContent || current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]) {
506	        drafts[prevKey] = {
507	          command: current.command,
508	          commandCursor: current.commandCursor,
509	          mentionIndex: current.mentionIndex,
510	          attachments: current.attachments,
511	          selection: this.activeSelection(current),
512	          dirtySelectionFields: current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.dirtySelectionFields ?? [],
513	          serverSelectionVersion: current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.serverSelectionVersion ?? null,
514	        };
515	      } else {
516	        delete drafts[prevKey];
517	      }
518	      if (nextDraft === EMPTY_COMPOSER_DRAFT) {
519	        delete drafts[nextKey];
520	      }
521	      return {
522	        ...current,
523	        selectedRunId: nextRunId,
524	        composerDraftsByRun: drafts,
525	        command: nextDraft.command,
526	        commandCursor: nextDraft.commandCursor,
527	        mentionIndex: nextDraft.mentionIndex,
528	        attachments: nextDraft.attachments,
529	        selectedConversationMode: nextDraft.selection.conversationMode,
530	        selectedCliAgent: nextDraft.selection.worker,
531	        selectedWorkerAccountId: nextDraft.selection.accountId,
532	        selectedModel: nextDraft.selection.model,
533	        selectedEffort: nextDraft.selection.effort,
534	        hydratedRunSelectionId: nextRunId && nextDraft.serverSelectionVersion ? nextRunId : null,
535	      };
536	    });
537	  }
538	
539	  setRenamingRunId(runId: string | null) {
540	    this.patch((current) => current.renamingRunId === runId
541	      ? {}
542	      : { renamingRunId: runId, renameDialogRevision: current.renameDialogRevision + 1 });
543	  }
544	
545	  setMovingRunId(runId: string | null) {
546	    this.patch((current) => current.movingRunId === runId
547	      ? {}
548	      : { movingRunId: runId, moveDialogRevision: current.moveDialogRevision + 1 });
549	  }

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:31:17.654Z

Now I'm tracing the new-conversation draft path: clicking "new session" calls selectRun(null), which pulls from composerDraftsByRun using the NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY, falling back to an empty composer draft with worker "auto" and an empty model string if nothing exists yet.

But the screenshot shows agent "Codex" rather than "Auto", so I need to figure out why — maybe a prior draft persisted with worker "claude" and model "claude-opus-5", or maybe "auto" resolves and displays as "Codex" somewhere. I should check how MobileComposerSettings renders the agent label, and look at the default selectedCliAgent state and resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange logic in HomeApp to understand the resolution.

10	/**
11	 * Unsent composer text is the one piece of client state a user cannot get back

16	  command: string;
17	  commandCursor: number;
18	  selection: ComposerSelection;
19	  dirtySelectionFields: ComposerSelectionField[];
20	  serverSelectionVersion: string | null;
21	  updatedAt: number;
22	};
23	
24	type PersistedComposerDraftsEnvelope = {
25	  version: 2;
26	  drafts: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft>;
27	};
28	
29	export type ComposerDraftSource = Pick<
30	  HomeUiState,
31	  | "command"
32	  | "commandCursor"
33	  | "selectedRunId"
34	  | "composerDraftsByRun"
35	  | "selectedConversationMode"
36	  | "selectedCliAgent"
37	  | "selectedWorkerAccountId"
38	  | "selectedModel"
39	  | "selectedEffort"
40	>;
41	
42	const DRAFT_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
43	const MAX_PERSISTED_DRAFTS = 50;
44	const MAX_SERIALIZED_LENGTH = 256_000;
45	// Writing to localStorage on every keystroke blocks the main thread, which is
46	// visible while typing on mobile. Steady-state edits coalesce into one trailing
47	// write, and every teardown signal flushes synchronously — that flush is the
48	// case this whole module exists for.
49	const WRITE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 400;
50	
51	export function composerDraftKey(selectedRunId: string | null) {
52	  return selectedRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
53	}
54	
55	function readPersistedDraft(value: unknown, now: number): PersistedComposerDraft | null {
56	  if (!value || typeof value !== "object") {
57	    return null;
58	  }
59	
60	  const record = value as Partial<PersistedComposerDraft>;
61	  if (typeof record.command !== "string") {
62	    return null;
63	  }
64	  if (typeof record.updatedAt !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(record.updatedAt)) {
65	    return null;
66	  }
67	  if (now - record.updatedAt > DRAFT_TTL_MS) {
68	    return null;
69	  }
70	
71	  const cursor = typeof record.commandCursor === "number" && Number.isFinite(record.commandCursor)
72	    ? record.commandCursor
73	    : record.command.length;
74	
75	  const selection = record.selection;
76	  if (!selection || typeof selection !== "object") return null;
77	  if (
78	    typeof selection.conversationMode !== "string"
79	    || typeof selection.worker !== "string"
80	    || typeof selection.accountId !== "string"
81	    || typeof selection.model !== "string"
82	    || typeof selection.effort !== "string"
83	  ) return null;
84	  const dirtySelectionFields = Array.isArray(record.dirtySelectionFields)
85	    ? record.dirtySelectionFields.filter((field): field is ComposerSelectionField => (
86	        field === "conversationMode" || field === "worker" || field === "accountId" || field === "model" || field === "effort"
87	      ))
88	    : [];
89	  if (record.command.length === 0 && dirtySelectionFields.length === 0) return null;
90	
91	  return {
92	    command: record.command,
93	    commandCursor: Math.min(Math.max(Math.trunc(cursor), 0), record.command.length),
94	    selection: selection as ComposerSelection,
95	    dirtySelectionFields,
96	    serverSelectionVersion: typeof record.serverSelectionVersion === "string" ? record.serverSelectionVersion : null,
97	    updatedAt: record.updatedAt,
98	  };
99	}
100	
101	export function parsePersistedComposerDrafts(raw: string | null, now: number) {
102	  if (!raw) {
103	    return {};
104	  }
105	
106	  let parsed: unknown;
107	  try {
108	    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
109	  } catch {
110	    // A corrupt entry is disposable: the next write replaces it.
111	    return {};
112	  }
113	
114	  const envelope = parsed as Partial<PersistedComposerDraftsEnvelope> | null;
115	  if (!envelope || typeof envelope !== "object" || envelope.version !== 2) {
116	    return {};
117	  }
118	  if (!envelope.drafts || typeof envelope.drafts !== "object") {
119	    return {};
120	  }
121	
122	  const drafts: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft> = {};
123	  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(envelope.drafts)) {
124	    const draft = readPersistedDraft(value, now);
125	    if (draft) {
126	      drafts[key] = draft;
127	    }
128	  }
129	  return drafts;
130	}
131	
132	/**
133	 * `composerDraftsByRun` only receives the active composer when the run
134	 * selection changes, so the live `command`/`commandCursor` fields have to be
135	 * folded in separately. Timestamps are carried over for unchanged drafts so an
136	 * untouched conversation does not keep refreshing its own expiry.
137	 */
138	export function collectComposerDrafts(
139	  state: ComposerDraftSource,
140	  now: number,
141	  previous: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft> = {},
142	) {
143	  const drafts: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft> = {};
144	
145	  const record = (
146	    key: string,
147	    command: string,
148	    commandCursor: number,
149	    selection: ComposerSelection,
150	    dirtySelectionFields: ComposerSelectionField[],
151	    serverSelectionVersion: string | null,
152	  ) => {
153	    if (command.length === 0 && dirtySelectionFields.length === 0) {
154	      return;
155	    }
156	    const prior = previous[key];
157	    const unchanged = prior
158	      && prior.command === command
159	      && prior.commandCursor === commandCursor
160	      && JSON.stringify(prior.selection) === JSON.stringify(selection)
161	      && JSON.stringify(prior.dirtySelectionFields) === JSON.stringify(dirtySelectionFields)
162	      && prior.serverSelectionVersion === serverSelectionVersion;
163	    drafts[key] = unchanged
164	      ? prior
165	      : { command, commandCursor, selection, dirtySelectionFields, serverSelectionVersion, updatedAt: now };
166	  };
167	
168	  for (const [key, draft] of Object.entries(state.composerDraftsByRun)) {
169	    record(key, draft.command, draft.commandCursor, draft.selection, draft.dirtySelectionFields, draft.serverSelectionVersion);
170	  }
171	  const activeStored = state.composerDraftsByRun[composerDraftKey(state.selectedRunId)];
172	  record(
173	    composerDraftKey(state.selectedRunId),
174	    state.command,
175	    state.commandCursor,
176	    {
177	      conversationMode: state.selectedConversationMode,
178	      worker: state.selectedCliAgent,
179	      accountId: state.selectedWorkerAccountId,
180	      model: state.selectedModel,
181	      effort: state.selectedEffort,
182	    },
183	    activeStored?.dirtySelectionFields ?? [],
184	    activeStored?.serverSelectionVersion ?? null,
185	  );
186	
187	  return drafts;
188	}
189	
190	export function serializeComposerDrafts(
191	  drafts: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft>,
192	  activeKey: string,
193	) {
194	  const stringify = (entries: Array<[string, PersistedComposerDraft]>) => JSON.stringify({
195	    version: 2,
196	    drafts: Object.fromEntries(entries),
197	  } satisfies PersistedComposerDraftsEnvelope);
198	
199	  // Newest first so eviction drops the stalest conversations, but the composer
200	  // the user is looking at is never a candidate.
201	  const ordered = Object.entries(drafts).sort((a, b) => {
202	    if (a[0] === activeKey) return -1;
203	    if (b[0] === activeKey) return 1;
204	    return b[1].updatedAt - a[1].updatedAt;
205	  });
206	
207	  let kept = ordered.slice(0, MAX_PERSISTED_DRAFTS);
208	  let serialized = stringify(kept);
209	  while (kept.length > 1 && serialized.length > MAX_SERIALIZED_LENGTH) {
210	    kept = kept.slice(0, -1);
211	    serialized = stringify(kept);
212	  }
213	  return serialized;
214	}
215	
216	export type ComposerDraftPersistenceOptions = {
217	  storage?: BrowserStorage | null;
218	  now?: () => number;
219	  debounceMs?: number;
220	};
221	
222	type DraftFlushTarget = {
223	  addEventListener: (type: string, listener: () => void) => void;
224	  removeEventListener: (type: string, listener: () => void) => void;
225	};
226	
227	/** Injectable so the teardown flush can be exercised without a DOM. */
228	export type ComposerDraftAttachOptions = {
229	  documentTarget?: (DraftFlushTarget & { readonly visibilityState: string }) | null;
230	  windowTarget?: DraftFlushTarget | null;
231	};
232	
233	export class ComposerDraftPersistence {
234	  private readonly explicitStorage: BrowserStorage | null | undefined;
235	  private readonly now: () => number;
236	  private readonly debounceMs: number;
237	  private manager: HomeUiStateManager | null = null;
238	  private lastWritten: Record<string, PersistedComposerDraft> = {};
239	  private lastSerialized: string | null = null;
240	  private writeTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
241	
242	  constructor(options: ComposerDraftPersistenceOptions = {}) {
243	    this.explicitStorage = options.storage;
244	    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
245	    this.debounceMs = options.debounceMs ?? WRITE_DEBOUNCE_MS;
246	  }
247	
248	  // The module singleton is constructed during SSR too, so the real storage
249	  // handle is resolved per call rather than captured at construction.
250	  private get storage() {
251	    return this.explicitStorage === undefined ? getBrowserLocalStorage() : this.explicitStorage;
252	  }
253	
254	  read() {
255	    const storage = this.storage;
256	    if (!storage) {
257	      return {};
258	    }
259	
260	    let raw: string | null = null;
261	    try {
262	      raw = storage.getItem(COMPOSER_DRAFTS_STORAGE_KEY);
263	    } catch {
264	      return {};
265	    }
266	
267	    this.lastSerialized = raw;
268	    this.lastWritten = parsePersistedComposerDrafts(raw, this.now());
269	    return this.lastWritten;
270	  }
271	
272	  /**
273	   * Restores saved text without clobbering anything already in the composer:
274	   * live state is newer than storage by definition, so it wins.
275	   */
276	  hydrate(manager: HomeUiStateManager) {
277	    const persisted = this.read();
278	    if (Object.keys(persisted).length === 0) {
279	      return;
280	    }
281	
282	    manager.update((current) => {
283	      const activeKey = composerDraftKey(current.selectedRunId);
284	      const composerDraftsByRun = { ...current.composerDraftsByRun };
285	      let restoredAnyRun = false;
286	
287	      for (const [key, draft] of Object.entries(persisted)) {
288	        if (key === activeKey || composerDraftsByRun[key]) {
289	          continue;
290	        }
291	        composerDraftsByRun[key] = {
292	          command: draft.command,
293	          commandCursor: draft.commandCursor,
294	          mentionIndex: 0,
295	          attachments: [],
296	          selection: draft.selection,
297	          dirtySelectionFields: draft.dirtySelectionFields,
298	          serverSelectionVersion: draft.serverSelectionVersion,
299	        };
300	        restoredAnyRun = true;
301	      }
302	
303	      const activeDraft = current.command.length === 0 ? persisted[activeKey] : undefined;
304	      if (!restoredAnyRun && !activeDraft) {
305	        return current;
306	      }
307	
308	      return {
309	        ...current,
310	        composerDraftsByRun,
311	        ...(activeDraft
312	          ? {
313	              command: activeDraft.command,
314	              commandCursor: activeDraft.commandCursor,
315	              selectedConversationMode: activeDraft.selection.conversationMode,
316	              selectedCliAgent: activeDraft.selection.worker,
317	              selectedWorkerAccountId: activeDraft.selection.accountId,
318	              selectedModel: activeDraft.selection.model,
319	              selectedEffort: activeDraft.selection.effort,
320	            }
321	          : {}),
322	      };
323	    });
324	  }
325	
326	  attach(manager: HomeUiStateManager, options: ComposerDraftAttachOptions = {}) {
327	    this.manager = manager;
328	    const unsubscribe = manager.subscribe(() => this.schedule());
329	
330	    const documentTarget = options.documentTarget === undefined
331	      ? (typeof document === "undefined" ? null : document)
332	      : options.documentTarget;
333	    const windowTarget = options.windowTarget === undefined
334	      ? (typeof window === "undefined" ? null : window)
335	      : options.windowTarget;
336	
337	    const detach = () => {
338	      unsubscribe();
339	      this.flush();
340	      this.manager = null;
341	    };
342	
343	    if (!documentTarget || !windowTarget) {
344	      return detach;
345	    }
346	
347	    // A mobile back gesture destroys the webview without running unload
348	    // handlers. `visibilitychange` is the last callback that reliably fires
349	    // before the process is torn down, so it — not `beforeunload` — is what
350	    // keeps a half-typed prompt from disappearing.
351	    const flushOnHide = () => {
352	      if (documentTarget.visibilityState === "visible") {
353	        return;
354	      }
355	      this.flush();
356	    };
357	    const flushNow = () => this.flush();
358	    // Window blur fires constantly on desktop; only a pending write is worth
359	    // the synchronous storage round trip.
360	    const flushPending = () => {
361	      if (this.writeTimer !== null) {
362	        this.flush();
363	      }
364	    };
365	
366	    documentTarget.addEventListener("visibilitychange", flushOnHide);
367	    // Page Lifecycle `freeze` fires on the document, not the window.
368	    documentTarget.addEventListener("freeze", flushNow);
369	    windowTarget.addEventListener("pagehide", flushNow);
370	    windowTarget.addEventListener("blur", flushPending);
371	
372	    return () => {
373	      documentTarget.removeEventListener("visibilitychange", flushOnHide);
374	      documentTarget.removeEventListener("freeze", flushNow);
375	      windowTarget.removeEventListener("pagehide", flushNow);
376	      windowTarget.removeEventListener("blur", flushPending);
377	      detach();
378	    };
379	  }
380	
381	  flush() {
382	    if (this.writeTimer !== null) {
383	      clearTimeout(this.writeTimer);
384	      this.writeTimer = null;
385	    }
386	    this.write();
387	  }
388	
389	  private schedule() {
390	    if (this.writeTimer !== null) {
391	      return;
392	    }
393	    this.writeTimer = setTimeout(() => {
394	      this.writeTimer = null;
395	      this.write();
396	    }, this.debounceMs);
397	  }
398	
399	  private write() {
400	    const manager = this.manager;
401	    const storage = this.storage;
402	    if (!manager || !storage) {
403	      return;
404	    }
405	
406	    const snapshot = manager.getSnapshot();
407	    const drafts = collectComposerDrafts(snapshot, this.now(), this.lastWritten);
408	    const serialized = Object.keys(drafts).length === 0
409	      ? null
410	      : serializeComposerDrafts(drafts, composerDraftKey(snapshot.selectedRunId));
411	
412	    if (serialized === this.lastSerialized) {
413	      return;
414	    }
415	
416	    if (serialized === null) {
417	      try {
418	        storage.removeItem(COMPOSER_DRAFTS_STORAGE_KEY);
419	      } catch {
420	        return;
421	      }
422	      this.lastWritten = {};
423	      this.lastSerialized = null;
424	      return;
425	    }
426	
427	    if (!safeSetBrowserStorageItem(storage, COMPOSER_DRAFTS_STORAGE_KEY, serialized)) {
428	      return;
429	    }
430	    this.lastWritten = drafts;
431	    this.lastSerialized = serialized;
432	  }
433	}
434	
435	export const composerDraftPersistence = new ComposerDraftPersistence();

147	function renderSettingControl(descriptor: MobileComposerSettingDescriptor, themeMode: "day" | "night") {
148	  if (descriptor.kind === "locked") {
149	    return <span className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">{descriptor.value}</span>;
150	  }
151	
152	  if (descriptor.kind === "model") {
153	    return (
154	      <ComposerModelPicker
155	        value={descriptor.value}
156	        options={descriptor.options}
157	        onChange={descriptor.onChange}
158	        themeMode={themeMode}
159	        disabled={descriptor.disabled}
160	      />
161	    );
162	  }
163	
164	  return (
165	    <ComposerSelect
166	      value={descriptor.value}
167	      options={descriptor.options}
168	      onChange={descriptor.onChange}
169	      themeMode={themeMode}
170	      ariaLabel={descriptor.ariaLabel}
171	      disabled={descriptor.disabled}
172	    />
173	  );
174	}

176	function getMobileSettingsSummary({

179	  selectedCliAgent,
180	  composerWorkerOptions,
181	  selectedModel,
182	  activeWorkerModelOptions,
183	  selectedEffort,
184	}: MobileComposerSettingDescriptorArgs) {
185	  const selectedHarnessLabel = shouldLockDirectWorker
186	    ? lockedDirectWorkerLabel
187	    : composerWorkerOptions.find((option) => option.value === selectedCliAgent)?.label ?? selectedCliAgent;
188	  const selectedModelLabel = activeWorkerModelOptions.find((option) => option.value === selectedModel)?.label ?? selectedModel;
189	  return `${selectedHarnessLabel} · ${selectedModelLabel} · ${selectedEffort}`;
190	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:31:46.604Z

The UI shows worker "codex" with model "claude-opus-5", a combination that shouldn't be reachable through setSelectedCliAgent since that function resolves to the first codex model automatically. I'm wondering if the worker got set to "codex" through a different path, like hydrateComposerSelection, that bypasses that fallback logic.

I'm considering another scenario: what if a user picks Claude + Opus 5 for a fresh conversation, sends it, then starts a "new session" — the draft restoration might still hold onto the stale worker/model pairing from before. But that would surface as "Claude Code" not "Codex", so that doesn't quite fit unless the Claude CLI isn't actually available on this runner, forcing activeAllowedWorkerTypes to exclude it and something falls back to codex while somehow retaining the old model selection. I want to trace through useRunSelectionEffects to see if that's the mechanism.

1	import { useEffect, useLayoutEffect, useRef } from "react";

12	const CONVERSATION_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD_PX = 8;
13	const CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX = 112;
14	const SCROLL_AREA_VIEWPORT_SELECTOR = '[data-slot="scroll-area-viewport"], [data-radix-scroll-area-viewport]';
15	
16	function textVersion(value: string | null | undefined) {
17	  const text = value ?? "";
18	  if (text.length <= 128) {
19	    return text;
20	  }
21	  return `${text.length}:${text.slice(0, 64)}:${text.slice(-64)}`;
22	}
23	
24	export function shouldConversationFollowLatest(
25	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
26	) {
27	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight - metrics.scrollTop <= CONVERSATION_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD_PX;
28	}
29	
30	export function shouldConversationShowOutputBelow(
31	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
32	) {
33	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight > CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX
34	    && !shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics);
35	}
36	
37	export function hasMeaningfulConversationOverflow(
38	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
39	) {
40	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight > CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX;
41	}
42	
43	/**
44	 * Following the latest output is cancelled by the reader scrolling UP, and by
45	 * nothing else.
46	 *
47	 * This used to end with a bare `shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics)`, which
48	 * meant content growing *beneath* the viewport also cancelled it — the reader
49	 * had not moved, but they were no longer within 8px of the bottom, so the app
50	 * decided they had chosen to stop following. `wasFollowing` keeps the intent
51	 * sticky; a reader who returns to the bottom re-arms it.
52	 */
53	export function shouldConversationKeepFollowingLatest(
54	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
55	  previousScrollTop: number,
56	  wasFollowing = false,
57	) {
58	  if (metrics.scrollTop < previousScrollTop) {
59	    return false;
60	  }
61	
62	  return wasFollowing || shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics);
63	}
64	
65	/**
66	 * Re-pin to the bottom when we are supposed to be following but have drifted
67	 * off it — i.e. content grew below the viewport after we positioned.
68	 *
69	 * This replaces a one-shot "retry the initial position" check that was gated on
70	 * `positionedRunId !== selectedRunId` and therefore could fire at most once per
71	 * run, during the very pass that marked the run positioned. If that single
72	 * attempt measured short for any reason, the conversation stayed stranded
73	 * mid-transcript with no way to recover. Making the condition a property of the
74	 * current geometry rather than a one-time flag means it self-corrects for every
75	 * cause of landing short — late layout, async content, or a transcript window
76	 * that grew after an earlier `loadOlder`.
77	 *
78	 * Terminates naturally: once pinned to the bottom
79	 * `shouldConversationFollowLatest` is true and this returns false.
80	 */
81	export function shouldConversationReanchorToLatest({
82	  selectedRunId,
83	  selectedRunHasOutput,
84	  shouldFollowLatest,
85	  metrics,
86	}: {
87	  selectedRunId: string | null;
88	  selectedRunHasOutput: boolean;
89	  shouldFollowLatest: boolean;
90	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">;
91	}) {
92	  return Boolean(
93	    selectedRunId
94	    && selectedRunHasOutput
95	    && shouldFollowLatest
96	    && hasMeaningfulConversationOverflow(metrics)
97	    && !shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics),
98	  );
99	}
100	
101	export function getConversationOutputVersion(
102	  selectedRunId: string | null,
103	  messages: MessageRecord[] | undefined,
104	  agents: AgentSnapshot[] | undefined,
105	) {
106	  if (!selectedRunId) {
107	    return "empty";
108	  }
109	
110	  const messageVersion = (messages ?? [])
111	    .filter((message) => message.runId === selectedRunId)
112	    .map((message) => `${message.id}:${message.createdAt}:${textVersion(message.content)}`)
113	    .join("|");
114	  const agentVersion = (agents ?? [])
115	    .map((agent) => {
116	      const outputEntriesVersion = (agent.outputEntries ?? [])
117	        .map((entry) => `${entry.id}:${entry.type}:${entry.status ?? ""}:${textVersion(entry.text)}`)
118	        .join(",");
119	      return `${agent.name}:${textVersion(agent.currentText)}:${textVersion(agent.lastText)}:${outputEntriesVersion}`;
120	    })
121	    .join("|");
122	
123	  return `${selectedRunId}::${messageVersion}::${agentVersion}`;
124	}
125	
126	export function hasSelectedRunMessageOutput(
127	  selectedRunId: string | null,
128	  messages: MessageRecord[] | undefined,
129	) {
130	  if (!selectedRunId) {
131	    return false;
132	  }
133	
134	  return (messages ?? []).some((message) => (
135	    message.runId === selectedRunId && Boolean(message.content.trim())
136	  ));
137	}
138	
139	interface UseRunSelectionEffectsProps {

155	}
156	
157	export function resolveRunComposerSelection(args: {
158	  run: RunRecord;
159	  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
160	}) {
161	  const preferredWorker = parseWorkerType(args.run.preferredWorkerType);
162	  const worker: ComposerWorkerOption = preferredWorker && args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(preferredWorker)
163	    ? preferredWorker
164	    : "auto";
165	  const modelWorker = preferredWorker ?? args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes[0] ?? "codex";
166	  return {

172	    effort: resolveComposerEffortLabel(args.run.preferredWorkerEffort) ?? DEFAULT_COMPOSER_EFFORT,
173	    accountId: args.run.preferredWorkerAccountId?.trim() || "auto",
174	  };
175	}
176

183	    run.preferredWorkerAccountId ?? null,

188	export function useRunSelectionEffects({

205	  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();

209	  const previousOutputVersionRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
210	  const instantPositionedRunIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
211	  const persistedReadMarkerRef = useRef<Map<string, string>>(new Map());
212	  const outputVersion = getConversationOutputVersion(selectedRunId, state.messages, state.agents);
213	  const selectedRunHasOutput = hasSelectedRunMessageOutput(selectedRunId, state.messages);
214	
215	  useEffect(() => {
216	    const viewport = scrollRef.current?.querySelector(SCROLL_AREA_VIEWPORT_SELECTOR) as HTMLDivElement | null;
217	    if (!viewport) {
218	      conversationMainManager.setHasOutputBelow(false);
219	      return;
220	    }
221	
222	    const positionAtLatest = (behavior: ScrollBehavior) => {
223	      viewport.scrollTo({

225	        behavior,
226	      });
227	      previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
228	      shouldFollowLatestRef.current = true;

252	      previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
253	      updateOutputBelowState();
254	    };
255	
256	    updateFollowState();

258

271

403	    }).catch((error) => {

405	      console.warn("Failed to persist conversation read marker:", error);
406	    });

More likely, the user selected a Claude conversation, its draft got model claude-opus-5, then opened a new session; if an earlier new-conversation draft already had worker=claude and model=claude-opus-5 persisted as dirty fields, the new session would load those values. This points to the core issue being in that resolution effect around where "auto" gets forced to a specific worker without properly syncing the model.

The real fix should mirror `setSelectedCliAgent`'s pattern, which uses `resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange` to keep the model in sync — unlike the current worker-change logic, which doesn't re-resolve the model and leaves a stale vendor's model behind. 1	import type { ComposerWorkerOption, ConversationModeOption, WorkerType } from "./types";
2	import { resolveComposerEffortValue, resolveSelectedWorkerModel } from "./utils";
3	
4	/**
5	 * The worker identity a send is launched with, frozen at the moment the user
6	 * hit send.
7	 *
8	 * Reading the live composer state from inside a mutation function is not safe.
9	 * `runCommand.onMutate` selects the conversation it is about to create, React
10	 * flushes that render before the awaited mutation function runs, and React
11	 * Query reads `options.mutationFn` lazily — so the request went out with
12	 * whatever the freshly selected (draftless) conversation had fallen back to,
13	 * which is the first model in the worker's catalogue. Carrying the selection
14	 * through the mutation variables makes the launch identity immune to any
15	 * re-render that happens between `mutate()` and the request.
16	 */
17	export type ComposerLaunchSelection = {
18	  conversationMode: ConversationModeOption;
19	  workerType: WorkerType | null;
20	  isAutoWorkerSelection: boolean;
21	  /** `null` means "keep the model this conversation already runs on". */
22	  model: string | null;
23	  effort: string;
24	  accountId: string | null;
25	  allowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
26	};
27	
28	export function resolveComposerLaunchSelection(args: {
29	  conversationMode: ConversationModeOption;
30	  selectedCliAgent: ComposerWorkerOption;
31	  selectedModel: string;
32	  selectedEffort: string;
33	  selectedWorkerAccountId: string;
34	  autoSelectedWorkerType: WorkerType | null;
35	  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
36	  activeWorkerModelValues: string[];
37	}): ComposerLaunchSelection {
38	  const explicitWorkerType = args.selectedCliAgent === "auto" ? null : args.selectedCliAgent;
39	  const isAutoWorkerSelection = explicitWorkerType === null;
40	  const workerType = explicitWorkerType ?? args.autoSelectedWorkerType;
41	  const resolvedModel = workerType
42	    ? resolveSelectedWorkerModel(workerType, args.selectedModel).trim()
43	    : "";
44	  // An explicit worker owns the model picker, so its value is asserted as-is.
45	  // Under "auto" the picker follows whichever worker the policy resolves to,
46	  // and a model left over from a different worker is not a choice for this one
47	  // — leaving it out keeps the conversation on the model it already ran.
48	  const model = isAutoWorkerSelection
49	    ? args.activeWorkerModelValues.includes(resolvedModel) ? resolvedModel : null
50	    : resolvedModel || null;
51	
52	  return {
53	    conversationMode: args.conversationMode,
54	    workerType,
55	    isAutoWorkerSelection,
56	    model,
57	    effort: resolveComposerEffortValue(args.selectedEffort),
58	    accountId: args.selectedWorkerAccountId === "auto" ? null : args.selectedWorkerAccountId,
59	    allowedWorkerTypes: isAutoWorkerSelection
60	      ? args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes
61	      : workerType
62	        ? [workerType]
63	        : args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes,
64	  };
65	}
66	
67	/**
68	 * The worker-preference fields of a create/send request body.
69	 *
70	 * `preferredWorkerModel` is omitted rather than sent as null when there is no
71	 * model to assert: the message route reads a present-but-empty value as "reset
72	 * this conversation's model", and that reset is what dropped a conversation
73	 * back onto the first model in the catalogue on its second message.
74	 */
75	export function buildLaunchPreferenceBody(selection: ComposerLaunchSelection) {
76	  return {
77	    preferredWorkerType: selection.workerType,
78	    preferredWorkerEffort: selection.effort,
79	    preferredWorkerAccountId: selection.accountId,
80	    allowedWorkerTypes: selection.allowedWorkerTypes,
81	    ...(selection.model ? { preferredWorkerModel: selection.model } : {}),
82	  };
83	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:32:51.113Z

This is a real launch bug, not just a labeling issue: when the effect forces the worker from "auto" to "codex" explicitly, isAutoWorkerSelection becomes false, so the launch would assert preferredWorkerModel as claude-opus-5 on a codex worker, meaning codex would actually start with claude-opus-5. The fix needs to happen where the worker gets programmatically changed in useRunSelectionEffects.

I need to re-resolve the model for the new worker there too, mirroring what setSelectedCliAgent does with resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange. But I have to be careful about the invariant that a conversation never changes its own model once selectedRunId is set — this branch only runs when there's no selected run or the run isn't loaded yet, which is usually a fresh composer, but I realize selectedRunId can be set while selectedRun is still missing (e.g., a freshly created run before the snapshot loads), so adding a model change here could risk violating that invariant for an actual conversation.

To stay safe, I'll gate the model re-resolution specifically on `!selectedRunId` rather than the broader condition already used for worker coercion, keeping the existing worker logic untouched and only adding model resolution for the truly new, run-less composer case. I also need to make sure these programmatic writes use `userEdited: false` so they don't get flagged as dirty selection fields like setComposerWorkerSelection does.

I'm extending `setComposerWorkerSelection` with an optional `userEdited` flag mirroring `setComposerSelectionField`, so when it's false the dirty fields tracking is skipped. Then I need to figure out how to get the worker model catalog into `useRunSelectionEffects` so it can resolve the model alongside the worker — checking whether HomeApp's `workerCatalogQuery` data or the view model already exposes this catalog to pass through.

I'm settling on fixing this at the worker-coercion site rather than the normalization effect, since whoever changes the worker should own the model change — consistent with how `setSelectedCliAgent` works and with the documented intent that an explicit worker change transfers ownership of the model picker to the new worker.

Now I want to verify this is the actual bug path. I should check the existing worker-coercion tests in run-selection-effects.test.ts, confirm the "New session" handler really calls selectRun(null) and resets drafts, and double-check that direct mode is indeed the default for new conversations (selectedConversationMode starts as "direct" in HomeApp), which would mean every new conversation hits this worker-lock logic.

If that's true, in direct mode worker "auto" always gets coerced to a concrete type, so the model gets asserted rather than skipped — meaning any stale selectedModel becomes reachable. I need to trace where that stale model value would come from for a fresh composer: checking `selectRun`, if drafts don't have an entry for "__new__" it should fall back to empty defaults, so the stale model must be coming from something persisted rather than copied from the previous run.

So for Codex + claude-opus-5 to appear, claude must have dropped out of activeAllowedWorkerTypes, or the user switched agents through the picker without the catalog loaded yet. Checking setSelectedCliAgent, if the worker catalog hasn't loaded when switching to Codex, resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange falls back to default codex options, which wouldn't include claude-opus-5 and would instead pick the first fallback option like "gpt-5.6-sol".

I'm also wondering if claude-opus-5 could sneak in via gateway-prefixed model discovery, but that feels like a stretch. More likely, the runner for this screenshot just doesn't have the Claude CLI installed, so claude gets filtered out of the available worker types entirely, leaving codex, gemini, and opencode as the active set while a stale claude-opus-5 selection persists in the draft.

That's the bug: when worker=claude gets coerced to codex because claude isn't in the allowed list, the model stays pinned at claude-opus-5 instead of being re-resolved -- which exactly matches the screenshot showing Agent Codex with an unavailable claude-opus-5 model. The "(unavailable)" label confirms the codex catalog was fully discovered and simply doesn't contain that model, and I notice the same defect exists in the omni-mode branch of that effect too. The fix is clear: re-resolve the model whenever this effect coerces the worker type. Now I want to check the tests and HomeApp for how this coercion is actually triggered.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:33:26.276Z

I'm working out the fix in useRunSelectionEffects by adding a workerModelCatalog prop and using resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange when coercing the worker. I'm also weighing whether to combine the worker and model field updates into a single atomic setter instead of two separate calls, to avoid non-atomic re-renders.

Simpler approach: extend `setComposerWorkerSelection(worker, model, { userEdited: false })` as a combined call, keeping the existing dirty-field semantics so user-driven changes via `setSelectedCliAgent` still mark fields dirty by default.

Writing worker resolution logic...

For codex, that fallback lands on gpt-5.6-sol, which seems fine since it matches what the user-driven path already does. I'm checking for loop risk — the effect only fires on an actual worker mismatch, and once resolved the selected agent matches, so it shouldn't re-trigger repeatedly; though I want to double check the edge case where a run exists but isn't in the snapshot yet, since applyWorker could still write a model in that scenario.

That would clobber the conversation's real model while its snapshot is still loading, and since the existing code already questionably clobbers the worker in that case, adding model clobbering on top would violate the "never change a conversation's own model" invariant. So I should gate the model re-resolution to only happen when there's no selectedRunId at all, leaving the existing worker-only behavior intact when a run id is present — though I'm second-guessing whether the worker coercion should even run in that snapshot-not-loaded case to begin with.

But there's a deeper issue: even with that gate, a persisted draft where the worker was previously coerced to something like codex with a mismatched model would never self-heal, since on reload the worker already matches and no change gets triggered, leaving the stale model stuck forever. Given the user's comment that this keeps recurring, I think I need to also add logic that heals a persisted new-conversation draft whose model doesn't belong to the currently selected agent, not just react to future worker changes.

I'm weighing where to put this — the existing normalization effect deliberately avoids touching saved session models, but that reasoning doesn't apply to a fresh composer where there's no session to preserve and the model simply must be runnable by the worker. I want to only trigger this when the catalog is fully loaded, mirroring the same completeness check used for the "(unavailable)" label, so I don't clobber selections mid-discovery. Though I'm second-guessing this too, since that unavailable label was intentionally designed to surface mismatches rather than silently overwrite them, so forcibly swapping could undermine that design goal.
