## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:33:54.414Z

Since the hook isn't tested with a renderer, I should pull the worker/model coercion logic into a standalone exported function that can be tested independently, ensuring a programmatic worker change reassigns the model picker the same way a user-driven change would.

Finishing the worker resolution logic: it falls back to "auto" if the selected agent isn't allowed, computes the effective worker type, and resolves the model, returning null only if both worker and model are unchanged. This nicely heals stale models in one place for the new-conversation composer, but I realize there's a risk—if the catalog hasn't fully loaded yet, the model resolution falls back to a limited fallback list, which could wrongly reset a legitimate model that exists in the full discovered catalog but not in that fallback set.

To avoid regressing the persisted-model behavior on fresh page loads, I think the safer approach is to only re-resolve the model when the worker itself actually changes, rather than whenever the catalog might be incomplete.

I'm considering passing a `catalogComplete` flag into the resolution function so it handles both the worker-change case and the stale-model-healing case in one place, but that flag is computed per-worker-type in HomeApp from the catalog query data, so I need to figure out how to thread it through to `useRunSelectionEffects` cleanly.

Actually I'm second-guessing whether the "already-persisted stale model" scenario is even a real concern, since drafts only persist when there are dirty selection fields or a non-empty command, and programmatic model changes mark `userEdited:false` so they shouldn't add dirty fields — though the draft could already have dirty fields from something else.

Tracing this through, the stale worker/model combo does end up persisting from an earlier user pick, and on reload the hydration keeps that stale pairing since the worker itself is still valid, so the model never gets corrected. That confirms it's a genuine sticky bug requiring both fix rules, so I need to implement the healing logic using catalog completeness — and now I'm weighing whether that healing rule should also fire when the worker itself hasn't changed but the catalog is complete.

I'm confirming the healing logic doesn't conflict with HomeApp's existing "(unavailable)" sentinel logic, which only applies for a different selected-run scenario. Then I lay out the implementation plan: adding an options parameter to setComposerWorkerSelection, exporting a pure coercion resolver from useRunSelectionEffects with new catalog props, wiring those props through HomeApp, and writing tests — and I start checking the type imports needed for the worker model catalog.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useRunSelectionEffects.ts",
  "old_string": "function runSelectionVersion(run: RunRecord) {",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Reconcile the new-conversation composer's worker/model pair.\n *\n * The worker half has to move on its own: direct mode cannot launch on \"auto\",\n * and a worker the runner does not offer is not a launchable choice. The model\n * half used to be left behind, which is how a brand new session came up reading\n * \"Codex · claude-opus-5 (unavailable)\" after the Claude CLI went missing from\n * the runner. It was worse than a wrong label — coercing \"auto\" to an explicit\n * worker is exactly what makes `resolveComposerLaunchSelection` assert the\n * model instead of dropping it, so the send went out asking Codex for an\n * Anthropic model.\n *\n * A worker change transfers ownership of the model picker, same rule as the\n * user's own worker change. When the worker is already right, the model is only\n * overridden once that worker's catalogue has finished discovery: mid-refresh\n * the fallback list is not evidence that a saved choice is gone, and the\n * composer says \"(unavailable)\" rather than silently swapping.\n *\n * Returns `null` when the composer is already consistent.\n */\nexport function resolveNewConversationWorkerSelection(args: {\n  composerMode: ComposerMode;\n  selectedCliAgent: ComposerWorkerOption;\n  selectedModel: string;\n  autoSelectedWorkerType: WorkerType | null;\n  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];\n  workerModelCatalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | undefined;\n  workerModelsRefreshing: boolean;\n}): { worker: ComposerWorkerOption; model: string } | null {\n  const fallbackWorker = args.autoSelectedWorkerType ?? args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes[0] ?? \"codex\";\n\n  let worker: ComposerWorkerOption;\n  if (args.composerMode === \"direct\") {\n    const current = args.selectedCliAgent === \"auto\" ? fallbackWorker : args.selectedCliAgent;\n    worker = args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(current) ? current : fallbackWorker;\n  } else if (args.selectedCliAgent !== \"auto\" && !args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(args.selectedCliAgent)) {\n    worker = \"auto\";\n  } else {\n    worker = args.selectedCliAgent;\n  }\n\n  const workerType = worker === \"auto\" ? fallbackWorker : worker;\n  const workerChanged = worker !== args.selectedCliAgent;\n  const catalogComplete = Array.isArray(args.workerModelCatalog?.[workerType]) && !args.workerModelsRefreshing;\n  if (!workerChanged && !catalogComplete) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  const model = resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange({\n    catalog: args.workerModelCatalog,\n    workerType,\n    selectedModel: args.selectedModel,\n  });\n  if (!workerChanged && model === args.selectedModel) {\n    return null;\n  }\n  return { worker, model };\n}\n\nfunction runSelectionVersion(run: RunRecord) {"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T14:35:04.759Z

I realize this is a behavior change beyond the minimal fix — previously the worker coercion ran even without a hydrated run, fighting the run's own selection once it loads, whereas returning early now lets the run's data take precedence, which seems more correct but I need to consider the implications further.

For a freshly created run where selectedRun is briefly null, adoptSelectionForCreatedRun already seeds the draft, so this new behavior is fine there. But since selectedModel isn't a hook prop and gets read straight from the snapshot, healing a stale persisted model only happens when the effect's dependencies change — so I need to make sure workerModelCatalog and workerModelsRefreshing are added to the deps list so the effect reruns and heals stale models once the catalog arrives on page load.

I also notice selectedCliAgent is used in the effect but missing from its deps, an existing gap I should fix, and I should add selectedModel as a proper hook prop rather than reading it from the snapshot so the dependency is honest — updating the interface and the call site accordingly.

## Claude · 2026-09-22T14:40:43.648Z

Fixed. The state in your screenshot — Agent **Codex**, Model **claude-opus-5 (unavailable)** — came from a coercion that moved the worker and left the model behind.

**What was happening** (`src/interface/home/useRunSelectionEffects.ts:310-322`, before the change): on the new-conversation composer, direct mode can't launch on "auto", and a worker the runner doesn't offer isn't launchable either, so the effect rewrote `worker`. It never touched `model`. Your persisted new-session draft had Claude + Opus 5; once the Claude CLI wasn't among the runner's available workers, the worker snapped to Codex and Opus 5 stayed.

The earlier fix (3a727242) covered the *user-driven* worker change — `setSelectedCliAgent` at `HomeApp.tsx:612` already calls `resolveComposerModelAfterWorkerChange`. The programmatic path never got the same treatment.

Two things made it stick and made it worse than a bad label:

- Once in that state, the worker matched, so nothing ever re-ran the coercion. The bad pair persisted to localStorage with its dirty markers and came back on every reload — hence "again".
- Coercing "auto" to an explicit worker is precisely what makes `resolveComposerLaunchSelection` (`composer-launch-selection.ts:48`) assert the model rather than drop it. Sending would have gone out asking Codex for `claude-opus-5`.

**The change:**

- `resolveNewConversationWorkerSelection` in `useRunSelectionEffects.ts` now resolves worker and model together. A worker change re-resolves the model, same rule as the user's own change. When the worker is already right, a mismatched model is only replaced once that worker's catalogue has finished discovery — mid-refresh the fallback list isn't evidence a saved choice is gone, so the "(unavailable)" label still does its job there.
- Applied atomically via `setComposerWorkerSelection(..., { userEdited: false })`, a new option that keeps the app's own reconciliation from being recorded as your choice and clears the dirty markers it just invalidated. That's what unsticks the persisted draft.
- The branch also used to fire for a run whose snapshot hadn't arrived yet. It now returns early there — that conversation's own saved model stays authoritative.

Tests: 7 cases for the resolver, 1 for the dirty-marker clearing, and the source-contract assertion in `tests/ui/composer-shell.test.ts` updated. Full suite passes except `tests/scripts/mac-install-workflow.test.ts`, which fails identically on a clean tree (it wants a built release).
